Those longer days just kind of took over my life, and I've been stuck feeling like this ever since.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself enjoying things you used to like before you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don't know... I guess I haven't really noticed. I mean, I still do the same stuff, like watching the game or tinkering in the garage, but... I don't really know if I enjoy it or not. It's just stuff to pass the time, you know? I haven't really thought about whether I like it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Like I was just saying... I haven't really stopped doing them. I guess I still have enough interest to turn the TV on or go out to the garage, so I wouldn't say I've lost interest completely. I just... I don't really know if I get any actual pleasure out of it anymore. It's just what I do to get through the evening.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that has given you a sense of pleasure?\n\nPatient: No, I really can't. I'm trying to think of something, but... no. Nothing comes to mind. Like I was saying, it's all just about getting through the day, so I haven't really felt that kind of good feeling in a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No. No, I haven't. I mean... I get tired of just going through the motions every day, sure, but I've never actually thought about ending my life. I just wake up, go to work, and get through it. That's not something I would do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than usual, or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, my body gets tired from work, but I'm not moving any slower than normal. And I don't have any trouble sitting still, either. I just kind of sit there when I get home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, my weight's been pretty much the same. I haven't noticed any big changes. I mean, my job keeps me pretty active, but yeah, I weigh about what I always have.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really have much to feel guilty about. I just go to work, do my job, and come home. I don't really sit around blaming myself for things. It's just the same routine every day, so there's not much to feel bad about in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts about death, even if you weren't thinking of ending your own life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I don't really think about dying or anything like that. I just kind of get through the day, you know? But no, no thoughts about death.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or anxious about things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't... I don't get anxious or worked up about stuff. Things just kind of are what they are, you know? I just do my job and that's about it. So, no, I wouldn't say I spend much time worrying about things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My sleep is pretty much the same as it's always been. I work outside all day doing landscaping, so by the time I get home, I'm usually just tired enough to sleep. I just go to bed at my normal time and wake up for work the next morning. So, no, nothing has really changed with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social situations or spending less time with friends and family since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I mean, I used to help organize a community garden and some local music gatherings, but lately, I've just been avoiding them. Like I was saying, it's all just about getting through the day, so I haven't really felt that kind of good feeling in a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No. No, I haven't. I mean... I get tired of just going through the motions every day, sure, but I've never actually thought about ending my life. I just wake up, go to work, and get through it. That's not something I would do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than usual, or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, my body gets tired from work, but I'm not moving any slower than normal. And I don't have any trouble sitting still, either. I just kind of sit there when I get home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, my weight's been pretty much the same. I haven't noticed any big changes. I mean, my job keeps me pretty active, but yeah, I weigh about what I always have.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really have much to feel guilty about. I just go to work, do my job, and come home. I don't really sit around blaming myself for things. It's just the same routine every day, so there's not much to feel bad about in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts about death, even if you weren't thinking of ending your own life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I don't really think about dying or anything like that. I just kind of get through the day, you know? But no, no thoughts about death.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or anxious about things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't... I don't get anxious or worked up about stuff. Things just kind of are what they are, you know? I just do my job and that's about it. So, no, I wouldn't say I spend much time worrying about things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My sleep is pretty much the same as it's always been. I work outside all day doing landscaping, so by the time I get home, I'm usually just tired enough to sleep. I just go to bed at my normal time and wake up for work the next morning. So, no, nothing has really changed with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social situations or spending less time with friends and family since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I mean, I used to help organize a community garden and some local music gatherings, but lately, I've just been avoiding them. People used to count on me for that stuff, and now they've had to step in and take over because I just don't go anymore. And it's bleeding into my work, too. I've started missing details on job planning and falling behind on the crew paperwork. I just... I don't have the energy for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... if I actually sit down and do something I like, I still enjoy it. Like having a cold beer on the porch or watching a football game, it still feels fine. It's not that I can't get any happiness out of things. I just... like I said, I just don't have the drive or the energy to go out and do much anymore. But the enjoyment is still there if I actually do something.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I don't really know if I get any actual pleasure out of it anymore. It's just what I do to get through the evening.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of anything recently that has given you a sense of pleasure?\n\nPatient: No, I really can't. Like I was saying, it's all just about getting through the day, so I haven't really felt that kind of good feeling in a long time.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about ending your life?\n\nPatient: No. No, I haven't. I mean... I get tired of just going through the motions every day, sure, but I've never actually thought about ending my life. I just wake up, go to work, and get through it. That's not something I would do.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower or faster than usual, or having trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I haven't noticed anything like that. I mean, my body gets tired from work, but I'm not moving any slower than normal. And I don't have any trouble sitting still, either. I just kind of sit there when I get home.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, my weight's been pretty much the same. I haven't noticed any big changes. I mean, my job keeps me pretty active, but yeah, I weigh about what I always have.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel guilty or blame yourself for things?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really have much to feel guilty about. I just go to work, do my job, and come home. I don't really sit around blaming myself for things. It's just the same routine every day, so there's not much to feel bad about in that way.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts about death, even if you weren't thinking of ending your own life?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I mean, I don't really think about dying or anything like that. I just kind of get through the day, you know? But no, no thoughts about death.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel worried or anxious about things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't... I don't get anxious or worked up about stuff. Things just kind of are what they are, you know? I just do my job and that's about it. So, no, I wouldn't say I spend much time worrying about things.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My sleep is pretty much the same as it's always been. I work outside all day doing landscaping, so by the time I get home, I'm usually just tired enough to sleep. I just go to bed at my normal time and wake up for work the next morning. So, no, nothing has really changed with that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social situations or spending less time with friends and family since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I mean, I used to help organize a community garden and some local music gatherings, but lately, I've just been avoiding them. People used to count on me for that stuff, and now they've had to step in and take over because I just don't go anymore. And it's bleeding into my work, too. I've started missing details on job planning and falling behind on the crew paperwork. I just... I don't have the energy for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that would normally bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that. I mean... if I actually sit down and do something I like, I still enjoy it. Like having a cold beer on the porch or watching a football game, it still feels fine. It's not that I can't get any happiness out of things. I just... like I said, I just don't have the drive or the energy to go out and do much anymore. But the enjoyment is still there if I actually do something.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your low, hollow feeling is mild, moderate, or severe most of the time?\n\nPatient: I'd say it's severe. It's... it's heavy. Just this constant, heavy feeling that doesn't really let up. It's there most of the time, just weighing me down.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy, but now you don't enjoy at all?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Like I was saying before, it's not that I don't enjoy things anymore. If I'm watching a game or just having a beer, I still like it. There isn't really anything I can think of that I used to like but just get nothing out of now. It's just the energy to do them that's missing, not the enjoyment itself.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: No, never anything like that. I don't hear voices or see things that aren't there. It's just... just my mood that's the problem.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're restless or have an inability to sit still, or on the other hand, do you feel like you're moving slower than you should be?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel restless or anything like that. I can sit still just fine. And I don't really feel like I'm moving slower than normal, either. I mean, my body works fine, it's just... I just feel tired and heavy inside, you know? But physically, no, I'm not pacing around or moving in slow motion.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're fidgety or have trouble staying still when you're sitting or lying down?\n\nPatient: No, like I just said, I don't really have any trouble sitting still. I'm not fidgety at all. I can lay on the couch or sit in a chair for hours, honestly. It's not a physical restlessness, it's just that lack of energy I mentioned.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're moving slower than other people or than you normally do?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I just answered that, but no, I'm not moving slower than normal. I'm just tired. My body moves the same as it always has."
